row read, and harmonic application needed for the $p$ target rows and the
source matrix $Y=[y_1\ \cdots\ y_r]$.  Apart from this declared response
charge, the query costs
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde O\!\left(
  \frac{T_{\rm mv}}{\sqrt\alpha}
  +rRL\operatorname{cvol}(F)
  +pr
 \right)
 +\mathcal C_{\rm val}(\mathcal C),
 \label{eq:coded-harmonic-query-work}
\end{equation}
where $R,L,p$ are from
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator} and
$\mathcal C_{\rm val}$ charges construction or maintenance of the exposed
boundary-membership dictionary, membership lookup, exact leaf evaluation, and
output.  In addition to retained response state, the explicit measurement,
decoder, and candidate arrays use $O(pr+BR)$ transient cells.  The
$rRL\operatorname{cvol}(F)$ term forms the direct bucket
contributions by incidence scans: each touched label updates only one bucket
and its $L$ bit counters per repetition.
Forming the anchor right-hand sides does not enumerate $U$: only columns with
$Q_{Av}\neq0$ contribute, and their identifiers and degrees are already in
the exposed anchor-cut record of
\cref{prop:exposed-incidence-sufficiency}.

For an adaptive trace, the same conclusion is valid event by event only if,
after the event tuple $(A,F,\widetilde K_F,E,m)$ is fixed, the event either
draws both fresh Gaussian source probes $g_t$ (hence $Y$) and a fresh
hash/sign target bank, or consumes an unused, unobserved block containing both
random layers and independent of the complete history.  All such blocks are
charged in $\mathcal C_{\rm harm}$.  A simultaneous whole-trace guarantee
additionally assigns summable budgets $\sum_j\delta_j\leq\delta$ and uses
\begin{equation*}
 r_j\geq c_{\rm sk}\xi^{-2}\log(4M_j/\delta_j),
 \qquad
 R_j=\left\lceil\log_4(2N/\delta_j)\right\rceil,
\end{equation*}
with every resulting logarithm included in the aggregate block charge.
Reusing either random layer after its answers have influenced a later event is
not justified by this corollary.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Condition on the current frontier.  The first Gaussian quadratic-form step in
\cref{lem:two-sided-harmonic-group-sketch}, unioned over the current leaves
and the root group $W$, gives, with failure probability at most $\delta/2$,
\begin{equation*}
 (1-\xi)a_-Z_F(\{v\})
 \leq\|X_{v,:}\|_2^2
 \leq(1+\xi)a_+Z_F(\{v\})
\end{equation*}
for every $v\in W$, and
\begin{equation*}
 \|X\|_F^2
 \leq(1+\xi)a_+Z_F(W)
 \leq(1+\xi)a_+|F|.
\end{equation*}
The last inequality is the source-aware leverage packing bound
\cref{eq:source-aware-normalized-mass-total}.  Hence
\cref{eq:coded-locator-mass-cap} holds with the $\theta,K$ in
\cref{eq:coded-harmonic-budget}, and every leaf in
\cref{eq:coded-harmonic-ambiguous-set} has sampled row energy at least
$\theta$.

Condition on this source event and draw the independent coded bank with
failure budget $\delta/2$.  Apply
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator}.  A final union bound proves the
no-false-negative claim.  Substitution of
\cref{eq:coded-harmonic-budget} into
\cref{eq:coded-locator-output,eq:coded-locator-row-count} proves the candidate
and target-row bounds.

For a source column $y_t$, the corresponding coded measurements are exactly
\begin{equation*}
 \frac1{\sqrt r}\Pi_WD_W^{-1/2}
      (Q\mathcal U_Fy_t)_W.
\end{equation*}
This is \cref{eq:frontier-contamination-subtraction}: the full measurement is
formed first and its known $F$ contribution is subtracted.  The
source-preparation cost is
\cref{eq:frontier-whitening-work}.  A scan of the frontier rows forms the
direct exterior terms; the implicit bank has at most $R(L+1)$ nonzeros per
label, so all $r$ source columns cost the second term of
\cref{eq:coded-harmonic-query-work}.  The decoder and its vector norms cost
$O(pr)$.  Every operation involving the anchor representation or final
candidate is, by definition, retained in the two explicit charges.  Finally,
  both random layers in a fresh unused block are independent of the history
  that fixes the current event, so the conditional union-bound argument of
\cref{thm:causal-orthogonal-response-sketches} applies event by event.
\end{proof}

The corollary closes the measurement-count question for one packed query:
the target rows are output-sensitive even at full cut rank and are shared by
all would-be hierarchy nodes.  It does not close the graph-local theorem,
because $\mathcal C_{\rm harm}$ may still include too many anchor solves,
stored old-face reads, or independent bank blocks.  The next lemma must bound
that aggregate charge through an epoch, or replace the harmonic bank by the
positive shifted-debt realization below.

\begin{theorem}[Certified adaptive reuse of multiscale coded banks]
\label{thm:certified-adaptive-coded-bank-reuse}
Fix an identifier universe of size $N\geq2$ and an upper bound $J_{\max}$ on
the number of packed queries in one fixed-anchor epoch.  At exact reference
query $j$, let
\begin{equation*}
 X_j\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal W_j\times r_j},
 \qquad
 \|X_j\|_F^2\leq K_j\theta_j,
 \qquad
 K_j\geq1,\quad \theta_j>0,
\end{equation*}
where $\mathcal W_j$ is any current subset of the identifier universe.  The
row-heavy superset is
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal G_j
 :=\{v\in\mathcal W_j:\|(X_j)_{v,:}\|_2^2\geq\theta_j\}
 \label{eq:certified-reference-row-heavy-set}
\end{equation}
and suppose the exact reference event set $\mathcal H_j$ satisfies
$\mathcal H_j\subseteq\mathcal G_j$.  Exact validation returns
$\mathcal H_j$ from any candidate set containing it, and the reference
controller's next state is a fixed function of this validated set and of
randomness independent of the target banks.  Rejected false candidates,
their order, and the raw bucket values do not change this state.

Put $\kappa_\ell:=2^\ell$.  For every capacity level that is used, draw one
target bank $\Pi^{(\ell)}$ from
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator} with mass parameter
$K=\kappa_\ell$ and failure parameter $\delta/J_{\max}$.  At query $j$, use
the smallest level $\ell(j)$ with $K_j\leq\kappa_{\ell(j)}$, decode its
candidate set, exactly validate every candidate, and update the controller
only with the validated set.  The same bank at a level is reused at every
later query assigned to that level.

Then, with probability at least $1-\delta$, no reference-heavy label is
missed at any query and the adaptive run follows exactly the reference state
trace.  If
\begin{equation*}
 K_{\max}:=\max_{j\leq J_{\max}}K_j,
\end{equation*}
the total number of distinct target rows over all capacity levels is
\begin{equation}
 P_{\rm epoch}
 =O\!\left(
  K_{\max}\log^2N\log(NJ_{\max}/\delta)
 \right),
 \label{eq:certified-multiscale-bank-rows}
\end{equation}
not the sum of the per-query row counts.  Query $j$ decodes and validates at
most
\begin{equation}
 O\!\left(
  K_j\log N\log(NJ_{\max}/\delta)
 \right)
 \label{eq:certified-multiscale-query-candidates}
\end{equation}
candidates, up to harmless rounding at $K_j=1$.

The levels need not be allocated in advance.  A level may be drawn lazily
the first time its capacity is requested, after the current query tuple is
fixed and before its measurements are inspected.  This requires no advice
about $K_{\max}$ and preserves the same row bound.

For the harmonic realization in
\cref{cor:shared-coded-harmonic-bank}, this proves that one harmonic target
bank per realized capacity level suffices throughout a certified adaptive
epoch.  The source probes may remain fresh with summable failure budgets as in
\cref{thm:causal-orthogonal-response-sketches}; on their simultaneous success
event, the truly ambiguous leaves are the required sets $\mathcal H_j$ and
are contained in the sampled row-heavy sets $\mathcal G_j$.  Their failure
probability is added separately.  The target-side response charge has the
exact decomposition
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal C_{\rm harm}^{\rm epoch}
 =\mathcal C_{\rm build}
   \bigl(A,\{\Pi^{(\ell)}\}_{\ell}\bigr)
  +\sum_j\mathcal C_{\rm apply}
   \bigl(A,\Pi^{(\ell(j))},Y_j\bigr),
 \label{eq:certified-harmonic-build-apply-split}
\end{equation}
where the first term builds each distinct row only once and the second term
charges every later old-face read and application.  Thus adaptive
correctness no longer forces one fresh harmonic bank build per event.  The
theorem does not bound either implementation term in
\cref{eq:certified-harmonic-build-apply-split}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First condition on the graph, the exact validation rule, and all randomness
other than the target banks.  This fixes the exact reference trace
recursively, including every
$(\mathcal W_j,X_j,\theta_j,K_j,\mathcal G_j,\mathcal H_j)$.  For a fixed reference query
$j$, its assigned bank has capacity
$\kappa_{\ell(j)}\geq K_j$, so
\begin{equation*}
 \|X_j\|_F^2\leq\kappa_{\ell(j)}\theta_j.
\end{equation*}
By \cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator}, the probability that this bank misses
a member of $\mathcal G_j$, and hence the probability that it misses a member
of $\mathcal H_j\subseteq\mathcal G_j$, is at most
$\delta/J_{\max}$.  The events may be
correlated because a level is reused; a union bound over the fixed reference
queries still gives total failure probability at most $\delta$.

It remains to justify using the reference queries in an adaptive execution.
If the execution ever leaves the reference trace, let $j$ be the first such
query.  Every earlier candidate set contained its complete reference event
set, and exact validation deleted every false candidate.  By the controller
hypothesis, all earlier state transitions therefore equal their reference
transitions.  Hence the query tuple at $j$ is exactly the fixed reference
tuple, and divergence can occur only if its assigned bank misses a member of
$\mathcal H_j$.  The probability of any first divergence is consequently
bounded by the preceding union.  This is the certified first-failure
coupling; no for-all adaptive-sketch guarantee is assumed.

For one level, substitute the common failure parameter
$\delta/J_{\max}$ into
\cref{eq:coded-locator-row-count}.  Since the realized capacities are powers
of two,
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{\ell:\ \kappa_\ell\leq2K_{\max}}\kappa_\ell
 <4K_{\max}.
\end{equation*}
This proves \cref{eq:certified-multiscale-bank-rows}.  Minimality of
$\ell(j)$ gives $\kappa_{\ell(j)}<2K_j$ unless $K_j=1$; apply
\cref{eq:coded-locator-output} to prove
\cref{eq:certified-multiscale-query-candidates}.

For lazy allocation, condition on the certified history through the first
request to a new level.  The current query tuple is already fixed, while the
new bank is fresh and independent.  The same first-failure argument applies
from that point onward.  Geometric summation is unchanged.  Finally, the
global identifier construction in
\cref{cor:shared-coded-harmonic-bank} lets the same $\Pi^{(\ell)}$ be
restricted to every later boundary.  Building each harmonic extension once
and charging every application separately gives
\cref{eq:certified-harmonic-build-apply-split}.
\end{proof}

The theorem removes the adaptive fresh-bank multiplier from
$\mathcal C_{\rm harm}$.  The remaining high-rank question is now strictly
implementation-side: represent the $P_{\rm epoch}$ harmonic rows and apply
the selected level after each light event within the desired aggregate old-face
ledger.

\begin{theorem}[Bidirectional harmonic application]
\label{thm:bidirectional-harmonic-application}
Fix an anchor $A$, put $U:=V\setminus A$, and let
$\Pi\in\mathbb R^{p\times U}$ be any target bank.  For a frontier
$F\subseteq U$ and a source matrix $Y\in\mathbb R^{F\times r}$, put
\begin{equation*}
 W:=Q_{AF}Y,
 \qquad
 \mathsf H(\Pi,Y)
 :=\Pi D_U^{-1/2}Q_{UA}Q_{AA}^{-1}W.
\end{equation*}
The old-face term in every column of
\cref{eq:transposed-response-sketch} has the two exactly equal realizations
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf H(\Pi,Y)
 =\left(Q_{AA}^{-1}Q_{AU}D_U^{-1/2}\Pi^T\right)^TW
 =\Pi D_U^{-1/2}Q_{UA}X,
 \qquad Q_{AA}X=W.
 \label{eq:bidirectional-harmonic-identity}
\end{equation}
Thus the target orientation solves $p$ anchor right-hand sides once and
stores their harmonic extensions, whereas the source orientation solves the
$r$ current right-hand sides and stores no target harmonic extension.

Suppose now that $\Pi$ is one signed hash-and-bit bank from
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator}, and let $\sigma$ be the number of its
nonzeros in one identifier column.  The source orientation can form
$\mathsf H(\Pi,Y)$ exactly, after solving for $X$, by one scan of the
anchor--exterior cut in
\begin{equation}
 O\!\left(pr+\sigma r\,\operatorname{cvol}(A)\right)
 \label{eq:source-oriented-coded-bank-work}
\end{equation}
arithmetic and hash operations.  Including the direct term and the known
frontier subtraction in
\cref{eq:transposed-response-sketch,eq:frontier-contamination-subtraction}
adds
$O(\sigma r\,\operatorname{cvol}(F))$ work.  The transient arrays use
$O(|A|r+pr)$ cells in addition to the exact multi-right-hand-side solve
state.  All degree reads are those of the exposed-incidence record in
\cref{prop:exposed-incidence-sufficiency}.  In particular, this realization
has no harmonic-bank build or hidden scan of the exterior, but it pays one
old-face cut scan and one anchor solve for every source block.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Symmetry of $Q_{AA}$ gives
\begin{align*}
 \left(Q_{AA}^{-1}Q_{AU}D_U^{-1/2}\Pi^T\right)^TW
 &=\Pi D_U^{-1/2}Q_{UA}Q_{AA}^{-1}W\\
 &=\Pi D_U^{-1/2}Q_{UA}X,
\end{align*}
which proves \cref{eq:bidirectional-harmonic-identity}.

For the work claim, initialize the $p\times r$ accumulator to zero.  While
scanning a cut incidence $(a,v)$ with $a\in A$ and $v\in U$, its contribution
to source column $t$ is
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{Q_{va}X_{a,t}}{\sqrt{d_v}}\,\Pi_{:,v}.
\end{equation*}
The identifier $v$ determines the $\sigma$ nonzero coordinates and signs of
$\Pi_{:,v}$, so the incidence costs $O(\sigma r)$.  There are at most
$\sum_{a\in A}d_a$ such incidences.  Initializing the accumulator costs
$O(pr)$, proving \cref{eq:source-oriented-coded-bank-work}.  Scanning $F$
and applying the same column rule forms the direct exterior term and the
frontier subtraction at the stated additional charge.  No other exterior
coordinate is read.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Online rent-or-buy orientation]
\label{cor:online-harmonic-rent-or-buy}
Use the certified adaptive banks of
\cref{thm:certified-adaptive-coded-bank-reuse}.  At level $\ell$, let
$p_\ell$ be its target-row count, let $\mathcal J_\ell$ be the queries
assigned to that level, and put
\begin{equation*}
 R_\ell^{\rm src}:=\sum_{j\in\mathcal J_\ell}r_j.
\end{equation*}
There is an online exact orientation rule, requiring neither the future query
count nor $K_{\max}$, whose total number of anchor right-hand sides at this
level is at most
\begin{equation}
 2\min\{p_\ell,R_\ell^{\rm src}\}.
 \label{eq:online-orientation-rhs-count}
\end{equation}
Consequently the epoch-wide number is
\begin{equation}
 O\!\left(
   \sum_\ell\min\{p_\ell,R_\ell^{\rm src}\}
 \right)
 \leq
 O\!\left(\min\left\{
   P_{\rm epoch},\sum_jr_j
 \right\}\right).
 \label{eq:epoch-orientation-rhs-count}
\end{equation}

The rule initially rents: it applies each query by the source orientation of
\cref{thm:bidirectional-harmonic-application}.  Immediately before the next
source block would make the cumulative rented width reach $p_\ell$, it buys:
it builds the $p_\ell$ target harmonic extensions and uses the target
orientation thereafter.  Measurements, decoded candidates, and exact
validation are unchanged, so the adaptive coupling of
\cref{thm:certified-adaptive-coded-bank-reuse} is preserved.

This closes the anchor-right-hand-side count, not the graph-local work bound.
Every rental still pays
\cref{eq:source-oriented-coded-bank-work}; after a purchase, every use still
pays the stored-response reads and multiplication in
\cref{eq:certified-harmonic-build-apply-split}.  These are the two remaining
implementation charges.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If the threshold is never reached, the rule rents
$R_\ell^{\rm src}<p_\ell$ right-hand sides.  Otherwise it rents fewer than
$p_\ell$ right-hand sides and then buys exactly $p_\ell$ target right-hand
sides.  In the latter case
$R_\ell^{\rm src}\geq p_\ell$, so both cases prove
\cref{eq:online-orientation-rhs-count}.  Summing over levels and using
$\sum_\ell p_\ell=P_{\rm epoch}$ gives
\cref{eq:epoch-orientation-rhs-count}.  Exactness is
\cref{eq:bidirectional-harmonic-identity}; the rent-or-buy decision depends
only on declared dimensions, never on bank contents.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Slack-normalized partial-gate packing]
\label{thm:slack-normalized-partial-gate-packing}
Let
$S_0\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq S_J$ be a monotone nested exact-face trace,
put $B_j:=S_{j+1}\setminus S_j$, and let
$z^j:=x[S_j]$.  Fix an exterior set $W\subseteq V\setminus S_J$ and define
its normalized exact demand at stage $j$ by
\begin{equation*}
 r_v^j:=\frac{c_v-Q_{vS_j}z^j_{S_j}}{\sqrt{d_v}},
 \qquad v\in W.
\end{equation*}
Fix a gate $g\in\mathbb R$ and a finite-band width $m>0$.  Retain separately
the checkpoint band $\{v:r_v^0>g-m\}$, and on the remaining labels put
\begin{equation*}
 W_-:=\{v\in W:r_v^0\leq g-m\},
 \qquad s_v:=g-r_v^0\geq m.
\end{equation*}

For each $j$, let $E_{B_j}$ embed $\mathbb R^{B_j}$ into
$\mathbb R^{S_{j+1}}$, and define
\begin{align*}
 H_j&:=E_{B_j}^TQ_{S_{j+1}S_{j+1}}^{-1}E_{B_j},\\
 u_j&:=E_{B_j}^TQ_{S_{j+1}S_{j+1}}
               (z^{j+1}-z^j)_{S_{j+1}},\\
 R_j&:=-D_{W_-}^{-1/2}Q_{W_-,S_{j+1}}
       Q_{S_{j+1}S_{j+1}}^{-1}E_{B_j}H_j^{-1/2},\\
 a_j&:=H_j^{1/2}u_j.
\end{align*}
Horizontally concatenate the $R_j$, vertically stack the $a_j$, and put
\begin{equation}
 R:=[R_0\ \cdots\ R_{J-1}],
 \qquad
 a:=(a_0^T,\ldots,a_{J-1}^T)^T,
 \qquad
 \mathcal E:=\|a\|_2^2
 =\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\|z^{j+1}-z^j\|_Q^2,
 \label{eq:slack-packed-response-factor}
\end{equation}
and $b:=\sum_j|B_j|$.  With
$D_s:=\operatorname{diag}(s_v:v\in W_-)$, the slack-normalized response
matrix
\begin{equation*}
 X:=D_s^{-1}R
\end{equation*}
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \|X\|_F^2\leq\frac{b}{m^2}.
 \label{eq:slack-packed-mass}
\end{equation}
If $\mathcal E>0$, every far label that crosses the gate belongs to one heavy
row set:
\begin{equation}
 \{v\in W_-:r_v^J>g\}
 \subseteq
 \left\{v\in W_-:\|X_{v,:}\|_2^2>\frac1{\mathcal E}\right\}.
 \label{eq:slack-crossing-heavy-containment}
\end{equation}
Moreover its charged degree volume obeys
\begin{equation}
 \vol\{v\in W_-:r_v^J>g\}
 \leq\frac{b\mathcal E}{m^2}.
 \label{eq:slack-crossing-volume}
\end{equation}

When $\mathcal E>0$, consequently applying
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator} to $X$ with
\begin{equation*}
 \theta:=\mathcal E^{-1},
 \qquad
 K:=\max\{1,b\mathcal E/m^2\}
\end{equation*}
produces, with its declared failure probability, a candidate superset of
every far-to-violating crossing using
\begin{equation}
 O\!\left(
  K\log^2N\log(N/\delta)
 \right)
 \label{eq:slack-packed-target-rows}
\end{equation}
target rows and
$O(K\log N\log(N/\delta))$ candidates.  The checkpoint transition band is
not claimed empty or free; its maintenance, every target-row application,
and exact candidate validation remain charged separately.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \cref{eq:dual-frontier-support},
$Q_{S_{j+1}S_{j+1}}(z^{j+1}-z^j)_{S_{j+1}}$ is supported on $B_j$.
Hence
\begin{equation*}
 (z^{j+1}-z^j)_{S_{j+1}}
 =Q_{S_{j+1}S_{j+1}}^{-1}E_{B_j}u_j,
 \qquad
 \|a_j\|_2^2=\|z^{j+1}-z^j\|_Q^2.
\end{equation*}
The exterior demand increments therefore factor as
\begin{equation}
 (r^J-r^0)_{W_-}
 =\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}R_ja_j
 =Ra.
 \label{eq:slack-packed-demand-factor}
\end{equation}
Monotonicity makes every coordinate of this vector nonnegative.

The squared norm of row $v$ of $R_j$ is
$(\chi_v^{B_j})^2/d_v$ in the notation of
\cref{lem:source-aware-leverage-packing}.  Therefore
\begin{equation*}
 \|X\|_F^2
 =\sum_{v\in W_-}\frac1{s_v^2d_v}
   \sum_j(\chi_v^{B_j})^2
 \leq\frac1{m^2}\sum_j|B_j|,
\end{equation*}
which proves \cref{eq:slack-packed-mass}.

If $r_v^J>g$, then
$(Ra)_v>s_v$.  Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\begin{equation*}
 1<\frac{|(Ra)_v|^2}{s_v^2}
 \leq\|X_{v,:}\|_2^2\|a\|_2^2,
\end{equation*}
proving \cref{eq:slack-crossing-heavy-containment}.  The same inequality and
$s_v\geq m$ imply
\begin{equation*}
 d_vm^2
 <\mathcal E\sum_j(\chi_v^{B_j})^2.
\end{equation*}
Sum over crossing labels and apply
\cref{eq:source-aware-leverage-sum} separately to every $j$ to obtain
\cref{eq:slack-crossing-volume}.  Finally,
\cref{eq:slack-packed-mass} is precisely the mass cap
$\|X\|_F^2\leq K\theta$; the coded-locator bounds follow directly.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Realized directional slack bank]
\label{cor:realized-directional-slack-bank}
Under the hypotheses of
\cref{thm:slack-normalized-partial-gate-packing}, define the single realized
normalized-change column
\begin{equation}
 y:=D_s^{-1}(r^J-r^0)_{W_-}=Xa.
 \label{eq:realized-slack-direction}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \|y\|_2^2\leq\frac{b\mathcal E}{m^2},
 \qquad
 \{v\in W_-:r_v^J>g\}\subseteq\{v:y_v>1\}.
 \label{eq:realized-slack-mass-crossing}
\end{equation}
Consequently the coded locator applied to the one-column matrix $y$, with
$\theta=1$ and
\begin{equation*}
 K:=\max\{1,b\mathcal E/m^2\},
\end{equation*}
contains every far crossing with its declared probability and uses
$\widetilde O(1+b\mathcal E/m^2)$ target rows and candidates.

This state is causal and append-only for a fixed target bank and fixed
checkpoint slacks.  At prefix $t$, put
\begin{equation*}
 y^{[t]}:=D_s^{-1}(r^t-r^0)_{W_-}.
\end{equation*}
Every scalar total/bit bucket measurement satisfies
\begin{equation}
 M(y^{[t+1]})=M(y^{[t]})
   +M\!\left(D_s^{-1}(r^{t+1}-r^t)_{W_-}\right).
 \label{eq:directional-bucket-append}
\end{equation}
Thus the decoder retains one scalar per bucket record and never stores or
replays an earlier correction direction.  In a harmonic implementation, the
target row again multiplies the column for $v$ by $1/s_v$, and each exact
correction supplies one new directional response update through
\cref{thm:bidirectional-harmonic-application}.  The online rent-or-buy rule
remains applicable.  Target-row installation and application, capacity
growth, checkpoint-slack refresh, transition-band state, and exact validation
remain charged.

Unlike \cref{cor:gaussian-compressed-slack-bank}, this corollary has no
Gaussian source layer: it locates crossings for the realized accumulated
correction rather than preserving correction-independent row energies for all
possible directions.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \cref{eq:slack-packed-mass,eq:slack-packed-response-factor},
\begin{equation*}
 \|y\|_2^2=\|Xa\|_2^2
 \leq\|X\|_F^2\|a\|_2^2
 \leq\frac{b\mathcal E}{m^2}.
\end{equation*}
If $r_v^J>g$, then
$y_v=(r_v^J-r_v^0)/s_v>1$, proving
\cref{eq:realized-slack-mass-crossing}.  The coded-locator conclusion follows
from \cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator}.  Finally, every bucket measurement
is linear in its input column, so
\cref{eq:directional-bucket-append} follows from
$y^{[t+1]}-y^{[t]}=D_s^{-1}(r^{t+1}-r^t)_{W_-}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Gaussian-compressed slack bank]
\label{cor:gaussian-compressed-slack-bank}
Under the hypotheses of
\cref{thm:slack-normalized-partial-gate-packing}, assume
$\mathcal E>0$ and let
$G\in\mathbb R^{b\times r}$ have independent standard Gaussian entries and
put
\begin{equation*}
 \widetilde X:=\frac1{\sqrt r}XG.
\end{equation*}
There is an absolute constant $c>0$ such that, for
$r\geq c\log(4N/\delta)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$ a coded bank
applied to $\widetilde X$ contains every crossing in
\cref{eq:slack-crossing-heavy-containment}, uses
\begin{equation*}
 \widetilde O\!\left(1+\frac{b\mathcal E}{m^2}\right)
\end{equation*}
target rows and candidates, and has only $r=O(\log(N/\delta))$ source
columns.  More explicitly one may take row threshold
$1/(2\mathcal E)$ and mass parameter
$K=\max\{1,3b\mathcal E/m^2\}$, with the coded-bank failure budget included
in the displayed probability.

In the harmonic realization, the target measurement matrix is the usual
hash-and-bit bank with its column for $v$ multiplied by $1/s_v$; the existing
transposed harmonic identity applies without change.  The Chebyshev source
whitening in \cref{thm:chebyshev-frontier-whitening} supplies the corresponding
Gaussian response columns up to its explicit constant sandwich.  Those
constant factors only rescale the declared threshold and capacity.  This
corollary bounds measurement dimension, not harmonic construction or
application work.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Standard Gaussian norm concentration and a union bound over at most $N$ rows
give, simultaneously,
\begin{equation*}
 \tfrac12\|X_{v,:}\|_2^2
 \leq\|\widetilde X_{v,:}\|_2^2
 \leq\tfrac32\|X_{v,:}\|_2^2
 \qquad(v\in W_-)
\end{equation*}
with failure probability at most $\delta/2$.  On this event every crossing
row has sampled energy greater than $1/(2\mathcal E)$, while
\begin{equation*}
 \|\widetilde X\|_F^2
 \leq\tfrac32\|X\|_F^2
 \leq\frac{3b}{2m^2}.
\end{equation*}
Apply \cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator} with the remaining failure budget
$\delta/2$.  The harmonic statement is linear substitution of the diagonal
slack scaling into
\cref{eq:transposed-harmonic-sketch,eq:transposed-response-sketch}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Append-only bucket-norm accumulation]
\label{lem:append-only-bucket-norm-accumulation}
Fix one hash-and-bit target bank and fixed row weights, and let source blocks
$X^{(1)},X^{(2)},\ldots$ arrive as new column blocks.  Put
$X^{[t]}:=[X^{(1)}\ \cdots\ X^{(t)}]$.  The exact decoder of
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator} for every prefix $X^{[t]}$ can be
maintained without retaining or replaying any earlier source column.

For every bucket/bit record, retain the two scalars
\begin{equation*}
 A_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}:=\|M_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 C_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}
 :=\|M_{s,b,0}^{[t]}-M_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}\|_2^2.
\end{equation*}
On arrival of block $t+1$, add the squared norms of its two corresponding
bucket vectors.  This uses $O(p)$ retained scalar cells and
$O(pr_{t+1})$ transient arithmetic after the new $p\times r_{t+1}$
measurements have been supplied.  At any prefix, comparing
$A_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}$ and $C_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}$ gives exactly the batched bit
test \cref{eq:coded-locator-bit-test}.

Thus fresh causal source blocks do not themselves force source-history
storage or replay.  The claim requires a fixed bank and fixed slack weights.
Allocating a larger bank after old blocks have been discarded, changing an
old row weight, constructing its harmonic representation, and supplying each
new measurement block are not free and are not covered by this lemma.  A new
label whose past row is identically zero may be installed prospectively
without source replay, but its response-state update remains charged.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For every bucket record, column concatenation gives
\begin{align*}
 \|M_{s,b,\ell}^{[t+1]}\|_2^2
 &=\|M_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}\|_2^2
   +\|M_{s,b,\ell}^{(t+1)}\|_2^2,\\
 \|M_{s,b,0}^{[t+1]}-M_{s,b,\ell}^{[t+1]}\|_2^2
 &=\|M_{s,b,0}^{[t]}-M_{s,b,\ell}^{[t]}\|_2^2\\
 &\quad+
   \|M_{s,b,0}^{(t+1)}-M_{s,b,\ell}^{(t+1)}\|_2^2.
\end{align*}
The summands occupy disjoint source coordinates, so there are no cross terms.
Induction proves that the retained scalars are the exact prefix norms used by
the decoder.  The work and storage bounds follow by counting the $p$ records
and the new source width.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Three-label slack reweighting obstructs norm-only partial
refresh]
\label{prop:three-label-norm-only-reweight-obstruction}
Consider one fixed repetition and one bucket of the hash-and-bit bank in
\cref{thm:coded-bucket-row-locator}.  Three colliding labels
$v_0,v_1,v_2$ have binary identifiers $00,01,10$ and realized signs $+1$.
Before a checkpoint-slack change, give every row weight one and let the old
source prefix have one scalar column.  The two possible row histories
\begin{equation}
 X=(-4,1,1)^T,
 \qquad
 X'=(-2,-1,-1)^T
 \label{eq:three-label-reweight-histories}
\end{equation}
produce exactly the same complete append-only bucket-norm state from
\cref{lem:append-only-bucket-norm-accumulation}:
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf N(X)=\mathsf N(X')
 :=(A_1,C_1,A_2,C_2)=(1,9,1,9).
 \label{eq:three-label-old-norm-state}
\end{equation}
Here bit one isolates $v_1$ and has complement $v_0+v_2$, while bit two
isolates $v_2$ and has complement $v_0+v_1$.

Now refresh the positive inverse-slack row weights from $(1,1,1)$ to
\begin{equation}
 w^+=(1,3,2),
 \qquad
 s^+=(1,1/3,1/2),
 \label{eq:three-label-new-slack-weights}
\end{equation}
where $s^+$ is the corresponding new slack vector.  In the first bit test,
the reweighted bit-bucket norm squared is $9$ for both histories, whereas the
reweighted complement norm squared is
\begin{equation}
 \|X_{v_0}+2X_{v_2}\|_2^2=4,
 \qquad
 \|X'_{v_0}+2X'_{v_2}\|_2^2=16.
 \label{eq:three-label-reweight-flip}
\end{equation}
Thus \cref{eq:coded-locator-bit-test} decodes that bit as one for $X$ and
zero for $X'$.  No deterministic function of the retained state
\cref{eq:three-label-old-norm-state}, the fixed bank metadata, and the new
weights can refresh the exact decoder for both histories.

This obstruction does not rely on negative row entries.  With two source
columns, the nonnegative histories
\begin{equation*}
 \begin{pmatrix}0&2\\1&0\\1&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 \begin{pmatrix}1&1\\0&1\\0&1\end{pmatrix}
\end{equation*}
have common old state $(1,5,1,5)$, while the same new weights make the first
comparison $9>8$ in the first history and $9<10$ in the second.

Three colliding labels are minimal for this exact state obstruction.  With at
most two labels, injective identifiers have a separating bit, whose two sides
give the two individual squared row norms.  Whenever another bit groups the
two rows, its old grouped norm also gives their inner product; when no bit
groups them, that inner product is not needed.  Hence every reweighted bit and
complement norm is reconstructible for one or two labels.

Name the attempted no-replay interface \textsc{NormOnlyWeightRefresh}.  It
stages one version/capacity header and the three positive weights, validates
the header, identifiers, weight positivity, and fixed-bank identity, and is
forbidden to read an old source cell, scan the anchor cut, apply a response,
or retain any prechange state beyond \cref{eq:three-label-old-norm-state}.
Universal exactness therefore forces it to reject before persistent mutation.
One such valid-but-unrefreshable request has the complete vector
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &(C_{\rm adj},R_{\rm adj},R_{\rm int},C_{\rm pre},C_{\rm ctl},C_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm resp},M_{\rm pers},M_{\rm tmp},C_{\rm mat},C_{\rm emit})\\
 &=\bigl(0,0,1,0,\Theta(1),0,0,\Theta(1),\Theta(1),0,1\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:three-label-reweight-rejection-eleven-vector}
\end{equation}
The control coordinate charges the capacity/weight header, all three weight
reads, and validation; the sole emission is an explicit rejection record.
Repeated rejected attempts add this vector attempt by attempt.

For comparison, \textsc{ReplayThreeWeightRefresh} is an exact accepted
micro-implementation when the three old row values in each of the two
nonnegative source columns above are available as six stored response cells.
It reads all six cells, forms the four reweighted two-cell bucket/complement
vectors, overwrites the four retained norms, performs the exact bit tests,
and emits one refreshed decoder record.  Charging the twelve numerical writes
(eight measurement cells and four norms), its complete vector is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &(C_{\rm adj},R_{\rm adj},R_{\rm int},C_{\rm pre},C_{\rm ctl},C_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm resp},M_{\rm pers},M_{\rm tmp},C_{\rm mat},C_{\rm emit})\\
 &=\bigl(0,0,1,0,\Theta(1),0,6,\Theta(1),\Theta(1),12,1\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:three-label-reweight-replay-eleven-vector}
\end{equation}
If those six old cells are not stored, a source-oriented reconstruction must
instead add its actual anchor-cut scan and anchor-response application to
$C_{\rm resp}$ (and any newly exposed adjacency to $C_{\rm adj}$ and
$R_{\rm adj}$); neither cost is hidden in
\cref{eq:three-label-reweight-replay-eleven-vector}.

This is an exact-real, one-bucket, named-state STOP.  It proves neither that
every weight refresh costs six response reads nor that arbitrary bank
reweighting needs quadratic state.  Retaining a richer Gram sketch, replaying
old source blocks, rebuilding from the exposed cut, using a collision-free
bank, changing the decoder, or proving a different support-safe trace all
remain possible.  It also says nothing about template mutation, an RPPR/KKT
chronology, terminal PPR/RPPR output, finite precision, coefficient bits, or
word-RAM complexity.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $X$, both isolated entries equal one and both complements equal $-3$;
for $X'$, both isolated entries equal $-1$ and both complements again equal
$-3$.  Squaring proves \cref{eq:three-label-old-norm-state}.  Under
$w^+$, the first isolated entry has magnitude three in either history, while
the two complements are $-2$ and $-4$, proving
\cref{eq:three-label-reweight-flip} and the indistinguishability claim.  The
two nonnegative matrix products are direct and give the displayed $8$ and
$10$ complement norms.

For minimality, absorb the known hash signs into the at-most-two row vectors.
An injective binary encoding supplies a bit separating them, so the retained
norms on that bit are their individual squared norms.  If a second bit groups
them on one side, the retained squared norm of the sum determines twice their
inner product after subtracting the two individual norms.  Positive diagonal
reweighting then evaluates every grouped norm by the polarization identity.
If no bit groups them, every measured side is empty or a singleton and
reweighting is immediate.  This also covers a one-label bucket.

Finally, a deterministic no-replay interface receives identical retained
inputs on the two histories but owes opposite exact first-bit replies, so it
cannot accept both.  The two resource vectors follow by counting the named
validation/rejection interface and the explicit six-read, twelve-write replay
schedule coordinate by coordinate.
\end{proof}

Together,
\cref{thm:slack-normalized-partial-gate-packing,cor:realized-directional-slack-bank,cor:gaussian-compressed-slack-bank,lem:append-only-bucket-norm-accumulation,prop:three-label-norm-only-reweight-obstruction}
close the information-level parts of an epoch-internal partial query.
Checkpoint slack normalization handles heterogeneous far-label margins with
one heavy-row threshold.  For the realized gate, one accumulated directional
column suffices and its scalar bucket measurements update linearly with no
source history.  If correction-independent row energies are needed instead,
Gaussian compression reduces the concatenated batch source dimension to
logarithmic size and a fixed coded bank retains exact prefix decoder norms
without storing old source columns.  That fixed-weight state has an exact
boundary: a three-label collision cannot be refreshed after a genuine slack
reweight from its retained norms alone.  A correct implementation must retain
richer prechange information, replay old response/source cells, rebuild from
the cut, use a different bank/decoder, or reject.  These results do not yet
bound installation or application of the slack-weighted harmonic target rows,
growth beyond a bank's declared capacity, a paid strategy for checkpoint
slack-weight changes, maintenance of the transition band, or exact
validation.  Those are now the entire response-side implementation ledger for
this route.

\begin{theorem}[Positive shifted-resolvent frontier ladder]
\label{thm:positive-resolvent-frontier-ladder}
Use the anchor $A$, frontier $F$, face
$T=A\mathbin{\dot\cup}F$, lift $\mathcal U_F$, and frontier Schur
complement $K_F$ of
\cref{lem:two-sided-harmonic-group-sketch}.  Fix
$\eta\in(0,1/8)$ and define
\begin{equation*}
 t_0:=\eta\sqrt\alpha,
 \qquad
 t_{j+1}:=(1+\eta)t_j,
 \qquad
 J:=\min\{j:t_j\geq\eta^{-1}\}.
\end{equation*}
For $0\leq j<J$, put
\begin{equation}
 \sigma_j:=t_{j+1}^2,
 \qquad
 \omega_j:=\frac2\pi(t_{j+1}-t_j),
 \qquad
 r_\eta(x):=\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\frac{\omega_j}{x+\sigma_j}.
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-ladder}
\end{equation}
Then all weights and shifts are positive,
\begin{equation}
 J=O\!\left(
  \eta^{-1}\left(\log\frac1\alpha+\log\frac1\eta\right)
 \right),
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-rung-count}
\end{equation}
and, uniformly for $x\in[\alpha,1]$,
\begin{equation}
 (1-4\eta)x^{-1/2}
 \leq r_\eta(x)\leq x^{-1/2}.
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-spectral-sandwich}
\end{equation}
The same quadrature has the accelerated work-mass ledgers
\begin{align}
 \sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\frac{\omega_j}{\alpha+\sigma_j}
 &\leq\frac1{\sqrt\alpha},
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-work-mass}\\
 \sum_{j=0}^{J-1}
  \omega_j\frac{1+\sigma_j}{\alpha+\sigma_j}
 &\leq\frac1{\sqrt\alpha}
   +\frac{2(1+\eta)}{\pi\eta}.
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-mass}
\end{align}

Let $E_F:\mathbb R^F\to\mathbb R^T$ be the coordinate embedding and
define the sparse shifted face matrices
\begin{equation}
 M_j:=Q_{TT}+\sigma_jE_FE_F^T.
 \label{eq:shifted-face-matrix}
\end{equation}
Block elimination gives the exact realization
\begin{equation}
 M_j^{-1}E_F
 =\mathcal U_F(K_F+\sigma_jI_F)^{-1},
 \qquad
 \mathcal U_Fr_\eta(K_F)
 =\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\omega_jM_j^{-1}E_F.
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-block-realization}
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every exterior group $C\subseteq V\setminus T$ and
$R_C:=D_C^{-1/2}Q_{CV}\mathcal U_F$,
\begin{equation}
 (1-4\eta)^2\norm{R_CK_F^{-1/2}}_F^2
 \leq\norm{R_Cr_\eta(K_F)}_F^2
 \leq\norm{R_CK_F^{-1/2}}_F^2.
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-group-sandwich}
\end{equation}
Thus rescaling the rational group mass by $(1-4\eta)^{-2}$ gives a
one-sided constant-factor upper oracle.

There is also a monotone accelerated coordinate ledger.  Put
$s_F:=(\sqrt{d_u})_{u\in F}$.  Then $K_F$ is Stieltjes,
$K_Fs_F\geq\alpha s_F$ coordinatewise, and
$(K_F)_{uu}\leq1$.  For $g\geq0$, solve all systems
\begin{equation*}
 (K_F+\sigma_jI)y_j=\omega_jg
\end{equation*}
by exact residual coordinate settlements, settling $u$ only when its current
nonnegative residual $p_{j,u}$ satisfies
$p_{j,u}/\sqrt{d_u}\geq\tau$.  If settling $u$ is charged $d_u$ units of
Schur-coordinate work, the total charge over every rung is at most
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\langle s_F,g\rangle}{\tau}
 \left(
  \frac1{\sqrt\alpha}
  +\frac{2(1+\eta)}{\pi\eta}
 \right).
 \label{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-work}
\end{equation}
For signed $g$, the same conclusion applies separately to $g^+$ and $g^-$.
This is an unconditional $\alpha^{-1/2}$ settlement count in the
Schur-coordinate model; graph work still has to charge the implementation of
one dense Schur coordinate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the Stieltjes integral
\begin{equation*}
 x^{-1/2}=\frac2\pi\int_0^\infty\frac{dt}{x+t^2}.
\end{equation*}
The function in the integral is decreasing.
\Cref{eq:positive-resolvent-ladder} is its right-endpoint rectangle rule on
$[t_0,t_J]$, so it never exceeds the integral.  On a geometric interval,
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{x+t_{j+1}^2}{x+t_j^2}\leq(1+\eta)^2.
\end{equation*}
Hence the relative rectangle-rule loss is at most
$1-(1+\eta)^{-2}\leq2\eta$.  The omitted lower and upper tails have relative
mass at most $2\eta/\pi$ each, because
$t_0=\eta\sqrt\alpha\leq\eta\sqrt x$ and
$t_J\geq\eta^{-1}\geq\sqrt x/\eta$.  This proves
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-spectral-sandwich}; the geometric-grid length
proves
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-rung-count}.

Evaluating the right rule at $x=\alpha$ proves
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-work-mass}.  Also
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_j\omega_j
 =\frac2\pi(t_J-t_0)
 \leq\frac{2(1+\eta)}{\pi\eta}.
\end{equation*}
Using
$\frac{1+\sigma_j}{\alpha+\sigma_j}
 \leq 1+\frac1{\alpha+\sigma_j}$ and
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-work-mass} proves
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-mass}.

The Schur complement of $M_j$ onto $F$ is
$K_F+\sigma_jI_F$.  The block inverse column is therefore
\begin{equation*}
 M_j^{-1}E_F
 =\begin{pmatrix}-Q_{AA}^{-1}Q_{AF}\\I_F\end{pmatrix}
  (K_F+\sigma_jI_F)^{-1},
\end{equation*}
which is
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-block-realization}.  Functional calculus in
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-spectral-sandwich}, followed by squaring,
congruence by $R_C$, and tracing proves
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-group-sandwich}.

It remains to prove the coordinate ledger.  Let
$s_T:=(\sqrt{d_u})_{u\in T}$.  Since $Q\sqrt d=\alpha\sqrt d$ and $T$ is
principal, $Q_{TT}s_T\geq\alpha s_T$.  Put
$h_A:=-Q_{AA}^{-1}Q_{AF}s_F$.  The $A$ block of the preceding inequality and
inverse positivity give $0\leq h_A\leq s_A$.  Because $Q_{FA}\leq0$,
\begin{equation*}
 K_Fs_F=Q_{FF}s_F+Q_{FA}h_A
 \geq Q_{FF}s_F+Q_{FA}s_A
 \geq\alpha s_F.
\end{equation*}
Schur complements of Stieltjes matrices are Stieltjes, and
$K_F\preceq Q_{FF}\preceq I$, proving the other two claims.

For rung $j$, use the residual potential
$\langle s_F,p_j\rangle$.  A coordinate settlement of $u$ by
$\Delta=p_{j,u}/((K_F)_{uu}+\sigma_j)$ preserves residual nonnegativity and
decreases the potential by
\begin{equation*}
 \Delta\bigl((K_F+\sigma_jI)s_F\bigr)_u
 \geq
 \tau d_u\frac{\alpha+\sigma_j}{1+\sigma_j}.
\end{equation*}
The initial potential is
$\omega_j\langle s_F,g\rangle$.  Sum the resulting work bounds and apply
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-mass} to obtain
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-work}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Delayed anchor debt is an exact sparse invariant]
\label{cor:shifted-resolvent-anchor-debt}
Fix one nonnegative source $g\in\mathbb R^F$.  For each rung, let
$x_j\in\mathbb R_{\geq0}^T$ be any partial state with nonnegative full residual
\begin{equation}
 d_j:=\omega_jE_Fg-M_jx_j\geq0.
 \label{eq:shifted-resolvent-debt}
\end{equation}
Then the rational frontier lift has the exact decomposition
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal U_Fr_\eta(K_F)g
 =\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}x_j
  +\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}M_j^{-1}d_j.
 \label{eq:shifted-resolvent-debt-invariant}
\end{equation}
Every term is nonnegative.  A full-system coordinate settlement preserves
\cref{eq:shifted-resolvent-debt-invariant}, touches only one row of the sparse
matrix $Q_{TT}$, and emits exterior response only through cut incidences of
that row.  No target harmonic row is needed.

More explicitly, if $d_j=(d_{j,A},d_{j,F})$, exact settlement of the current
anchor debt adds $Q_{AA}^{-1}d_{j,A}$ to the anchor state and replaces the
frontier residual by
\begin{equation}
 \overline d_{j,F}
 :=d_{j,F}-Q_{FA}Q_{AA}^{-1}d_{j,A}\geq0.
 \label{eq:shifted-resolvent-schur-debt}
\end{equation}
This is precisely the residual of the shifted Schur system
$(K_F+\sigma_jI)y_j=\omega_jg$.  Thus the positive resolvent ladder represents
a dense Schur coordinate settlement by sparse local propagation plus delayed
nonnegative anchor debt.  The remaining graph-universal obligation is to
aggregate those debt settlements at the Schur-coordinate bound
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-work}; it is no longer the algebraic
construction of target Gaussian harmonic rows.  Literal row-by-row cleanup
does not suffice, as \cref{prop:one-edge-shifted-debt-obstruction} proves.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By definition,
$M_jx_j+d_j=\omega_jE_Fg$.  Apply $M_j^{-1}$, sum over $j$, and use
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-block-realization} to prove
\cref{eq:shifted-resolvent-debt-invariant}.  Each $M_j$ is Stieltjes, so its
inverse is nonnegative.  A coordinate residual settlement zeros the selected
coordinate and adds nonnegative residual to its graph neighbors; the diagonal
shift creates no new incidence.  This proves the locality and positivity
claims.

For the final statement, add $Q_{AA}^{-1}d_{j,A}$ to the anchor block.  The
anchor residual becomes zero, while the frontier residual changes by
$-Q_{FA}Q_{AA}^{-1}d_{j,A}$, proving
\cref{eq:shifted-resolvent-schur-debt}.  Inverse positivity and
$Q_{FA}\leq0$ show that this residual remains nonnegative.  Block elimination
then identifies it with the shifted Schur residual.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Literal sparse debt settlement restores the repeated
factor]
\label{prop:one-edge-shifted-debt-obstruction}
Fix $\eta\in(0,1/8)$ and consider the first rung of
\cref{thm:positive-resolvent-frontier-ladder}.  On the one-edge graph
$o\mathord{-}v$, take $A=\{o\}$ and $F=\{v\}$.  Put
\begin{equation*}
 a:=\frac{1+\alpha}{2},
 \qquad
 h:=\frac{1-\alpha}{2},
 \qquad
 c_\eta:=(1+\eta)^2\eta^2.
\end{equation*}
Then
\begin{equation}
 M_0=
 \begin{pmatrix}a&-h\\-h&a+c_\eta\alpha\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 \omega_0=\frac{2\eta^2}{\pi}\sqrt\alpha.
 \label{eq:one-edge-first-shifted-rung}
\end{equation}
Start from zero with residual $d^{(0)}=\omega_0e_v$, and implement the rung
only by exact coordinate residual settlements on the sparse matrix $M_0$.
For $0<\alpha\leq1/10$, reducing
$\|d^{(N)}\|_\infty$ to at most $\omega_0/4$ requires
\begin{equation}
 N\geq \frac{\log 2}{5\alpha}
 \label{eq:one-edge-sparse-shifted-lower-bound}
\end{equation}
row settlements.  Every settlement costs one adjacency-row touch.

In contrast, the Schur complement of $M_0$ onto $F$ is the scalar
$K_F+c_\eta\alpha$, so one exact Schur-coordinate settlement makes its
residual zero.  Thus the $O(1/\sqrt\alpha)$ ledger in
\cref{eq:positive-resolvent-coordinate-work} cannot be transferred to graph
work merely by replacing every dense Schur-coordinate operation with literal
sparse exact pushes and postponing the induced anchor residual.  Some
additional aggregation primitive---for example elimination, block response,
accelerated transport, or dynamic sparsification---is necessary.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
At every time exactly one residual coordinate is nonzero, so every
nontrivial exact coordinate schedule is forced to alternate between $v$ and
$o$.  Define
\begin{equation*}
 \mu:=\frac{h}{a+c_\eta\alpha},
 \qquad
 \lambda:=\frac{h^2}{a(a+c_\eta\alpha)}.
\end{equation*}
Direct substitution in the exact coordinate update gives, for every $k\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
 d^{(2k)}=\omega_0\lambda^ke_v,
 \qquad
 d^{(2k+1)}=\omega_0\mu\lambda^ke_o.
 \label{eq:one-edge-shifted-alternation}
\end{equation}
For $\eta<1/8$, $c_\eta<1/50$.  If $\alpha\leq1/10$, then
$\mu\geq1/2$.  Moreover,
\begin{equation*}
 1-\lambda
 =\frac{\alpha+a c_\eta\alpha}{a(a+c_\eta\alpha)}
 \leq5\alpha\leq\frac12,
\end{equation*}
and hence
\begin{equation*}
 -\log\lambda
 \leq\frac{1-\lambda}{\lambda}
 \leq10\alpha.
\end{equation*}
If termination occurs after $2k$ settlements,
\cref{eq:one-edge-shifted-alternation} implies $\lambda^k\leq1/4$.
If it occurs after $2k+1$ settlements, $\mu\geq1/2$ implies
$\lambda^k\leq1/2$.  In either case the displayed logarithmic bound yields
\cref{eq:one-edge-sparse-shifted-lower-bound}.

Finally, block elimination gives the scalar frontier operator
$K_F+c_\eta\alpha$.  An exact coordinate settlement in a scalar system is
the exact solve, proving the separation.  The proposition concerns this
literal sparse realization only; it is not a lower bound for richer response
or block primitives.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Aggregate fixed-face debt flush with signed recurrence and
certified exterior]
\label{thm:aggregate-chebyshev-debt-flush}
Work in the exact-real algebraic-cell model.  Fix throughout the operation the
face and its partition $T=A\mathbin{\dot\cup}F$, the exposed blocks
$Q_{TT}$ and $Q_{WT}$, where
\begin{equation*}
 W:=\{v\notin T:Q_{vT}\neq0\},
\end{equation*}
and the complete positive shifted ladder
$\{(\sigma_j,\omega_j,M_j):0\leq j<J\}$ of
\cref{thm:positive-resolvent-frontier-ladder}.  No vertex moves between
$A,F,W$ during this operation, and no rung, shift, or weight changes.

For the scalar primitive, let additive nonnegative debt fragments
$d_j^{(\ell)}\in\mathbb R_{\geq0}^T$ arrive on rung $j$ and put
\begin{align}
 \bar d_j&:=\sum_\ell d_j^{(\ell)},
 &H&:=\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\norm{\bar d_j}_2,
 &\mathcal C_{\rm frag}
 &:=\sum_{j,\ell}\operatorname{nnz}(d_j^{(\ell)}).
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-scalar-data}
\end{align}
Thus $\mathcal C_{\rm frag}$ charges every incoming coordinate read, write,
and aggregation; repeated writes to the same aggregate coordinate are not
discarded from the ledger.  The exact correction is
\begin{equation}
 z:=\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}M_j^{-1}\bar d_j\geq0.
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-exact-correction}
\end{equation}
If $H=0$, take $z=\widetilde z=0$.  If $H>0$, then for every declared
energy budget $\delta>0$ there are Chebyshev semi-iterates
$\widetilde z_j$, computed only by sparse $M_j$-matrix--vector products,
such that, for $\widetilde z:=\sum_j\widetilde z_j$,
\begin{equation}
 \norm{z-\widetilde z}_{Q_{TT}}\leq\delta.
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-energy-certificate}
\end{equation}
Let $m_j$ be the number of products used on rung $j$, with $m_j=0$ when
$\bar d_j=0$ and $m_j\geq1$ otherwise, and put
$\mathfrak M_1:=\sum_jm_j$.  The allocation in the
proof gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak M_1
 =O\!\left(
  J+
  \frac{J+(1+\eta)^2/\eta^2}{\sqrt\alpha}
  \log\!\left(2+\frac{H}{\delta\sqrt\alpha}\right)
 \right).
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-chebyshev-products}
\end{equation}

For $r$ nonnegative columns, fragments
$D_j^{(\ell)}\in\mathbb R_{\geq0}^{T\times r}$ aggregate to
$\bar D_j$.  Write $\bar d_{j,q}$ for column $q$, set
\begin{equation}
 H_q:=\sum_j\norm{\bar d_{j,q}}_2,
 \qquad
 Z_q:=\sum_jM_j^{-1}\bar d_{j,q},
 \qquad
 \mathfrak M_r:=\sum_{q=1}^r\sum_jm_{j,q},
 \qquad
 \mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{(r)}
 :=\sum_{j,\ell}\operatorname{nnz}(D_j^{(\ell)}),
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-multicolumn-data}
\end{equation}
and give column $q$ its own budget $\delta_q>0$ when $H_q>0$.
Then
\begin{align}
 \norm{Z_q-\widetilde Z_q}_{Q_{TT}}&\leq\delta_q,
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-multicolumn-error}\\
 \mathfrak M_r
 &\leq\sum_{q:H_q>0}
 O\!\left(
  J+\frac{J+(1+\eta)^2/\eta^2}{\sqrt\alpha}
  \log\!\left(2+\frac{H_q}{\delta_q\sqrt\alpha}\right)
 \right).
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-multicolumn-products}
\end{align}
There is no free block-matrix multiplication: its exact-cell charge is the
sum over all $r$ columns.  For signed source columns, split every fragment
before aggregation as
$D_j^{(\ell)}=D_j^{(\ell),+}-D_j^{(\ell),-}$ and run the two nonnegative
streams separately.  With budgets $\delta_q/2$ for each sign,
\begin{equation}
 Z_q:=Z_q^+-Z_q^-,
 \qquad
 \widetilde Z_q:=\widetilde Z_q^+-\widetilde Z_q^-,
 \qquad
 \norm{Z_q-\widetilde Z_q}_{Q_{TT}}\leq\delta_q.
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-signed-split}
\end{equation}
Its product count is
$\mathfrak M_{2r}:=\sum_{q,j}(m_{j,q}^++m_{j,q}^-)$, namely the
right side of \cref{eq:aggregate-debt-multicolumn-products} applied to the
$2r$ sign streams with their actual masses $H_q^\pm$.  In particular, it is
at most twice the uniform $r$-column bound evaluated with the half-budgets
$\delta_q/2$ and the corresponding sign-stream masses.  No cancellation
between the two debt streams is credited before certification.  Its fragment coordinate is
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{(\pm)}
 :=\sum_{j,\ell}
 \left(
  \operatorname{nnz}(D_j^{(\ell),+})
  +\operatorname{nnz}(D_j^{(\ell),-})
 \right);
\end{equation*}
when one signed entry is split canonically, exactly one of its two sign cells
is nonzero, but separately supplied positive and negative fragments are all
charged.

For every exterior label and every output column, the exact normalized dual
response satisfies the mathematical interval
\begin{equation}
 -\frac{Q_{vT}\widetilde Z_q}{\sqrt{d_v}}-\delta_q
 \ \leq
 -\frac{Q_{vT}Z_q}{\sqrt{d_v}}
 \ \leq
 -\frac{Q_{vT}\widetilde Z_q}{\sqrt{d_v}}+\delta_q,
 \qquad v\notin T.
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-boundary-interval}
\end{equation}
The same formula covers the signed split through
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-signed-split}.  The simultaneous quantifier in
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-boundary-interval} is a mathematical certificate;
it does not materialize a single interval center, find a violating label, or
implement \textsc{BoundaryBounds} for free.

Here is one exhaustively charged realization of the stronger, non-output-
sensitive operation \textsc{AllBoundaryFlush}.  Scan every row of $T$, then
query every newly revealed incident boundary degree in a second adjacency
batch, retain the exposed record, and process the $p$ nonnegative streams sequentially
($p=r$ for nonnegative columns and $p=2r$ for a signed split), retain the $r$
signed or unsigned output accumulators, scan the stored cut once per output
column, materialize and test both endpoints for every $v\in W$, and emit all
of those intervals plus one certificate record per output column.  Let
$e_\partial(T)$ be the number of cut incidences, let $I_{\rm frag}$ be the
number of externally ordered fragment-arrival stages (zero when fragments
are generated internally), and let $\mathcal C_{\rm frag}$ count all $p$
streams.  In the shared order, this implementation has
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &(C_{\rm adj},R_{\rm adj},R_{\rm int},C_{\rm pre},C_{\rm ctl},C_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm resp},M_{\rm pers},M_{\rm tmp},C_{\rm mat},C_{\rm emit})\\
 =\bigl(&O(\operatorname{cvol}(T)),O(1),I_{\rm frag}+1,0,
 O(\mathcal C_{\rm frag}+\mathfrak M_p+pJ+r|W|),\\
 &O(\mathfrak M_p\operatorname{cvol}(T)),
 O(r(e_\partial(T)+|W|)),\\
 &O(\operatorname{cvol}(T)+\mathcal C_{\rm frag}+pJ+r|T|+|W|),
 O(|T|+|W|),\\
 &O(\mathfrak M_p|T|+r(|T|+|W|)),
 \Theta(r(|W|+1))\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-eleven-vector}
\end{equation}
At most two adjacency rounds first reveal the rows and cut labels and then
query the newly known boundary degrees.  The $C_{\rm ctl}$ coordinate includes
ladder/coefficient generation,
fragment aggregation, stopping tests, interval arithmetic, dictionary
membership, and threshold/band classification.  Sparse Chebyshev products
and their vector recurrences are in $C_{\rm rec}$; cut contractions and
normalized response-query computation are in $C_{\rm resp}$.  The
$C_{\rm mat}$ coordinate explicitly retains all full-face recurrence writes,
the $r$ accumulated corrections, and the materialized interval endpoints.
The stated sequential schedule gives the displayed peak workspace; a block
schedule may use $O(p|T|)$ temporary cells instead.  The sole final
interaction is the all-boundary certificate reply.  It emits no terminal
PPR/RPPR vector.  If the caller co-requests the materialized correction
$\widetilde Z$, add $r|T|$ cells to $C_{\rm emit}$; any separate
\textsc{Finalize} stage and its output are additional.
Here ``test'' or ``validate'' means checking the certified endpoints against
the declared gate and transition band.  Exact refinement of a label whose
interval still intersects the band is a separate response/control,
materialization, and possibly interaction charge.

Thus the scalar fixed-face recurrence work is
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde O_\eta\!\left(
  \frac{\operatorname{cvol}(T)}{\sqrt\alpha}
  +\mathcal C_{\rm frag}
 \right),
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-flush-work}
\end{equation}
and the corresponding $r$-column and signed-split bounds replace the first
term by respectively $r$ and at most $2r$ times that sparse-sweep term, with
the actual $\mathfrak M_r$ or $\mathfrak M_{2r}$ authoritative.  The cut,
interval, materialization, memory, round, and emission coordinates remain as
displayed in \cref{eq:aggregate-debt-eleven-vector}; they are not hidden by
the soft-order scalar summary.  No coefficient-bit, rounding-stability,
finite-precision, online face-mutation, output-sensitive partial-flush, or
terminal PPR/RPPR guarantee follows from this theorem.

The interior Chebyshev recurrence may be signed, but only the certified
intervals---never its intermediate signs---may control admission.  In
particular, on the one-edge instance of
\cref{prop:one-edge-shifted-debt-obstruction}, a scalar Chebyshev flush reduces
the first-rung residual by a factor four in $O(1/\sqrt\alpha)$ complete
two-row sparse sweeps.  Literal nonnegative exact-coordinate settlement still
requires $\Omega(1/\alpha)$ individual row touches.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Every shifted face matrix obeys
\begin{equation}
 \alpha I_T\preceq M_j\preceq(1+\sigma_j)I_T.
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-shifted-spectrum}
\end{equation}
Put $\kappa_j:=(1+\sigma_j)/\alpha$ and
$\theta_j:=(\sqrt{\kappa_j}-1)/(\sqrt{\kappa_j}+1)$.  The minimax
Chebyshev residual polynomial $p_m$, normalized by $p_m(0)=1$, satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \norm{p_m(M_j)h}_2
 \leq2\theta_j^m\norm{h}_2
 \leq2\exp\!\left(-\frac{2m}{\sqrt{\kappa_j}}\right)\norm{h}_2
 \label{eq:aggregate-debt-chebyshev-residual}
\end{equation}
for every $h$.  Since $p_m(0)=1$, the polynomial
$q_{m-1}(x):=(1-p_m(x))/x$ has degree $m-1$.  Define
\begin{equation*}
 \widetilde z_j:=q_{m-1}(M_j)\bar d_j
\end{equation*}
This vector is computed by the standard Chebyshev semi-iteration without applying an
inverse.

Skip every rung with $\bar d_j=0$.  On the remaining rungs allocate
\begin{equation*}
 \delta_j:=\delta\frac{\norm{\bar d_j}_2}{H}.
\end{equation*}
Choose $m_j$ so that the right side of
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-chebyshev-residual} is at most
$\delta_j\sqrt\alpha$.  This requires
\begin{equation*}
 m_j=O\!\left(
  1+\sqrt{\frac{1+\sigma_j}{\alpha}}
  \log\!\left(2+\frac{H}{\delta\sqrt\alpha}\right)
 \right).
\end{equation*}
For $e_j:=M_j^{-1}\bar d_j-\widetilde z_j$, the residual certificate and
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-shifted-spectrum} give
\begin{equation*}
 \norm{e_j}_{Q_{TT}}
 \leq\norm{e_j}_{M_j}
 \leq\frac{\norm{M_je_j}_2}{\sqrt\alpha}
 \leq\delta_j.
\end{equation*}
Summing and using $\sum_j\delta_j=\delta$ proves
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-energy-certificate}.  Each $M_j$ is Stieltjes, so
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-exact-correction} is nonnegative even though the
Chebyshev semi-iterates need not be.

It remains to sum the product counts.  Since
$\sqrt{1+\sigma_j}=\sqrt{1+t_{j+1}^2}\leq1+t_{j+1}$ and the grid is
geometric,
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\sqrt{1+\sigma_j}
 \leq J+\sum_{k=1}^{J}t_k
 \leq J+\frac{(1+\eta)^2}{\eta^2}.
\end{equation*}
Here minimality of $J$ gives $t_J<(1+\eta)/\eta$.  This proves
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-chebyshev-products}.  One product by $M_j$ scans the
sparse rows of $Q_{TT}$ and adds only the diagonal shift on $F$, proving
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-flush-work} and the stated source-column charge.

Apply \cref{cor:boundary-leverage-one} on the face $T$ to the total error in
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-energy-certificate}.  It gives, simultaneously as a
mathematical statement,
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{|Q_{vT}(z-\widetilde z)|}{\sqrt{d_v}}
 \leq\delta,
\end{equation*}
which is \cref{eq:aggregate-debt-boundary-interval}.  Applying the scalar
construction independently to every column proves
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-multicolumn-error,eq:aggregate-debt-multicolumn-products};
the triangle inequality proves \cref{eq:aggregate-debt-signed-split}.  The
explicit scans, scalar operations, stored cells, and emitted records described
in the statement give \cref{eq:aggregate-debt-eleven-vector} coordinate by
coordinate.

Finally, on the one-edge first rung,
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-shifted-spectrum} has condition number
$O(1/\alpha)$.  Taking $m=O(1/\sqrt\alpha)$ in
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-chebyshev-residual} makes the Euclidean residual at
most one quarter of its initial value.  One matrix--vector product touches
both sparse rows, which is the complete-sweep accounting asserted above and
is compatible with the individual-row lower bound in
\cref{prop:one-edge-shifted-debt-obstruction}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Geometric one-flush ledger]
\label{cor:geometric-aggregate-debt-flush}
Suppose fixed-face debt intervals end on nested faces
$T_0\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq T_K$ satisfying
$\operatorname{cvol}(T_{k+1})\geq
(1+\gamma)\operatorname{cvol}(T_k)$ for fixed $\gamma>0$.  Aggregate every
coordinate fragment during interval $k$ on one fixed partition
$T_k=A_k\mathbin{\dot\cup}F_k$ and its fixed ladder, and invoke
\cref{thm:aggregate-chebyshev-debt-flush} once at its end, with fixed
$\eta$, a fixed number $r$ of output columns, and logarithmically bounded
certificate parameters.  Let $p=r$ for nonnegative columns and $p=2r$ for a
signed positive/negative split.  If $\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}$ is the
total charged number of incoming fragment-coordinate writes, then all sparse
recurrences and fragment aggregation cost
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde O_{\eta,\gamma}\!\left(
  \frac{p\operatorname{cvol}(T_K)}{\sqrt\alpha}
  +\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}
 \right).
 \label{eq:geometric-aggregate-debt-flush-work}
\end{equation}
For the named \textsc{AllBoundaryFlush} realization, expose each newly
admitted row once, keep the cut dictionary incrementally, and let
$I_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}$ be the total number of external fragment-arrival
stages.  If $\mathfrak M_{p,k}$ is the product count at checkpoint $k$, put
\begin{align*}
 \mathcal W_{\rm rec}
 &:=\sum_{k=0}^K\mathfrak M_{p,k}\operatorname{cvol}(T_k),
 &\mathcal W_{\rm mat}
 &:=\sum_{k=0}^K\mathfrak M_{p,k}|T_k|,\\
 W_\Sigma&:=\sum_{k=0}^K|W_k|,
 &E_\Sigma&:=\sum_{k=0}^Ke_\partial(T_k),
\end{align*}
where $W_k$ is the current graph boundary.  Its complete vector is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &(C_{\rm adj},R_{\rm adj},R_{\rm int},C_{\rm pre},C_{\rm ctl},C_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm resp},M_{\rm pers},M_{\rm tmp},C_{\rm mat},C_{\rm emit})\\
 =\bigl(&O(\operatorname{cvol}(T_K)),O(K+1),
 I_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}+K+1,0,\\
 &O(\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}
      +\textstyle\sum_k\mathfrak M_{p,k}+pJ(K+1)+rW_\Sigma),
 O(\mathcal W_{\rm rec}),O(r(E_\Sigma+W_\Sigma)),\\
 &O(\operatorname{cvol}(T_K)+\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\max}
       +pJ+r|T_K|+\max_k|W_k|),\\
 &O(|T_K|+\max_k|W_k|),
 O(\mathcal W_{\rm mat}+r\textstyle\sum_k(|T_k|+|W_k|)),
 \Theta(r(W_\Sigma+K+1))\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:geometric-aggregate-debt-eleven-vector}
\end{equation}
Here $\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\max}$ is the largest unflushed interval
storage.  Geometric growth bounds
$W_\Sigma,E_\Sigma,\sum_k|T_k|$ by
$O_\gamma(\operatorname{cvol}(T_K))$.  Hence the full all-boundary scans fit
the product scale, but they occur only at these checkpoints.

This is an exact-real implementation theorem only for intervals whose support
decisions are already fixed.  It gives no finite-precision or bit bound and
does not make the online gate sleep safely.  Between checkpoints the remaining
alternatives are an output-sensitive partial-flush reporter or a different
proved support-safe trace.  The next proposition rules out literal geometric
sleep even on a path.  Individual debt pieces and the one-edge
alternating-push factor are no longer separate fixed-face obstructions.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Sum the scalar-stream bounds in
\cref{eq:aggregate-debt-eleven-vector} and use the geometric-series bound
$\sum_k\operatorname{cvol}(T_k)\leq
(1+1/\gamma)\operatorname{cvol}(T_K)$.  Fragment writes are disjoint charged
events and add directly.  Every boundary label and cut incidence is bounded by
the charged volume of its current face, so the same geometric sum proves the
remaining claims and every coordinate of
\cref{eq:geometric-aggregate-debt-eleven-vector}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A frozen notched sun obstructs eager exact slack vectors]
\label{prop:notched-sun-eager-slack-vector-obstruction}
Work in the exact-real algebraic-cell model.  Fix $n\geq5$ and
$\eta\in(0,1/8)$.  Use the notched-double-sun graph of
\cref{prop:notched-double-sun-loss-rank}, but not its prescribed changing-face
trace.  Freeze instead
\begin{equation*}
 T=A\mathbin{\dot\cup}F,
 \qquad
 A=\{u_1,\ldots,u_n\},
 \qquad
 F=\{f_1,\ldots,f_n\},
 \qquad
 W=\{w_1,\ldots,w_n\}.
\end{equation*}
Thus $\operatorname{cvol}(T)=7n+2$, the exposed cut from $T$ to $W$ has
exactly the $n$ incidences $u_iw_i$, and the normalized anchor--report block
has rank $n$.  Fix the complete ladder of
\cref{thm:positive-resolvent-frontier-ladder}.  Put
\begin{equation*}
 \vartheta:=\frac{1-\alpha}{2},
 \qquad
 c_\eta:=(1+\eta)^2\eta^2,
 \qquad
 \sigma_0=c_\eta\alpha,
 \qquad
 M:=M_0=Q_{TT}+\sigma_0E_FE_F^T.
\end{equation*}
During the fixed-face epoch, event $k$ contributes the single nonnegative
first-rung fragment $e_{f_k}$ and no fragment on another rung.  Define its
exact normalized exterior response column
\begin{equation}
 h^{(k)}:=-D_W^{-1/2}Q_{WT}M^{-1}e_{f_k}.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-frozen-response-column}
\end{equation}
Every entry of every $h^{(k)}$ is strictly positive for every
$\alpha\in(0,1)$.  For fixed $n$ and $\eta$, as
$\vartheta\downarrow0$,
\begin{align}
 h_k^{(k)}
 &=\frac{\vartheta^2}{d_{u_k}(1+c_\eta)}
   +O_{n,\eta}(\vartheta^3),
 \label{eq:notched-sun-frozen-matching-response}\\
 h_i^{(k)}&=O_{n,\eta}(\vartheta^3),
 \qquad i\neq k.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-frozen-off-response}
\end{align}

Use the strict gate and transition band
\begin{equation}
 g:=\vartheta^{5/2},
 \qquad
 m:=\frac12g,
 \label{eq:notched-sun-frozen-gate-band}
\end{equation}
and initialize all report demands at zero.  There is
$\alpha_{n,\eta}^{\rm eager}<1$ such that, whenever
$\alpha\in(\alpha_{n,\eta}^{\rm eager},1)$, the cumulative response
\begin{equation*}
 y^{[k]}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^kh^{(\ell)}
\end{equation*}
has the exact delta-only trace
\begin{equation}
 \{i:y_i^{[k]}>g\}=\{1,\ldots,k\},
 \qquad
 y_i^{[k]}\leq g-m\quad(i>k).
 \label{eq:notched-sun-frozen-delta-trace}
\end{equation}
Thus event $k$ emits exactly the newly crossing label $w_k$, and every other
unreported label remains outside the transition band.  This is a fixed-face
debt/query trace.  It is not an RPPR load, KKT admission chronology, or
support-evolution claim.

Now name the representation and query policy
\textsc{EagerExactSlack}.  For every certified nonnegative stream and every
not-yet-emitted label, it stores one separately addressed exact cell equal to
the current response, eagerly adds the new exact column
\cref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-response-column} to all of those cells, tests
each resulting slack against \cref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-gate-band}, and only
then returns the delta labels plus a certificate that all unreported labels
are at most $g-m$.  Since every response-column entry is nonzero, event $k$
must read and overwrite at least $n-k+1$ exact response cells per stream.
Consequently its epoch-internal exact response-state charge is
\begin{equation}
 p\sum_{k=1}^n(n-k+1)
 =\frac{p n(n+1)}2,
 \label{eq:notched-sun-eager-state-write-lower-bound}
\end{equation}
where $p=r$ for $r$ nonnegative output columns and $p=2r$ when $r$ signed
columns are certified by positive/negative splitting.  The scalar charge is
the case $p=r=1$.

The fragment and output interfaces can be kept equally explicit.  For $r$
nonnegative columns, supply one unit fragment in every column at every event,
so $\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}=rn$.  For signed column $q$, separately
supply $2e_{f_k}$ to its positive stream and $e_{f_k}$ to its negative
stream.  Their signed difference is again $e_{f_k}$, but certification uses
both streams and credits no cancellation before their two error bounds are
combined; hence $\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}=2rn$.  In both cases the
external delta interface emits the $r$ records $(q,w_k)$ and one
all-remaining-safe certificate per output column at event $k$, for
$C_{\rm emit}=\Theta(rn)$.  The signed example is deliberately chosen so the
final combined gate has the same trace while the two certified streams both
change densely.

There is a literal exact-cell implementation matching the dense scale of
\cref{eq:notched-sun-eager-state-write-lower-bound}.  Eliminate the $F$
leaves from $M$.  The remaining anchor matrix is a weighted cycle with one
chord and a diagonal correction.  Break the cycle and chord into a constant-
rank update of a tridiagonal path factor; exact tridiagonal elimination plus
the constant-size Schur correction uses $\Theta(n)$ cells and gives one exact
source solve, leaf recovery, and complete $W$ contraction in $\Theta(n)$
cells.  Process the $p$ streams sequentially and retain all exact slack cells.
Including one initial scan of the frozen face, a second degree-query round for
the newly exposed report labels, all $n$ fragment/query interactions, ladder
generation, membership, exact solves, slack tests, state writes, and delta
replies, its complete vector is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &(C_{\rm adj},R_{\rm adj},R_{\rm int},C_{\rm pre},C_{\rm ctl},C_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm resp},M_{\rm pers},M_{\rm tmp},C_{\rm mat},C_{\rm emit})\\
 =\bigl(&\Theta(n),2,n,\Theta(n+J),
 \Theta(\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}+pn^2+pJ),0,
 \Theta(pn^2),\\
 &\Theta(pn+n+J),O(n+p),\Theta(pn^2),\Theta(rn)\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:notched-sun-eager-eleven-vector}
\end{equation}
Here $C_{\rm resp}$ charges every exact factor application and cut
contraction, while $C_{\rm mat}$ separately charges the materialized response
columns and overwritten exact slack-vector cells.  Persistent memory contains
the cyclic factor, ladder, membership state, and all $p$ slack vectors; the
displayed scratch bound uses a sequential stream schedule.  No terminal
PPR/RPPR vector is emitted.

One aggregate call after all fragments still has the scalar recurrence bound
of \cref{eq:aggregate-debt-flush-work}; for the present near-one-$\alpha$
family it is $\widetilde O_\eta(n+\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma})$ before the
separately charged all-boundary realization.  Any beginning or ending
\textsc{AllBoundaryFlush} remains charged by
\cref{eq:geometric-aggregate-debt-eleven-vector} and may still be scheduled
only at geometric face checkpoints.  It does not pay the $n$ intervening
delta replies.  Therefore \textsc{EagerExactSlack} fails the Round-013 GO
test: it spends $\Theta(pn^2)$ between checkpoints for only $\Theta(rn)$
delta/certificate output.  This STOP is only for the named eager, separately
addressed exact-vector representation and query policy.  It does not refute
an implicit cyclic transfer, a packed coded query, a scale-truncated state,
on-demand exact validation, a sparsifier, or any output-sensitive partial
flush.  No coefficient-bit, rounding-stability, finite-precision, or word-RAM
conclusion follows.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Every $w_i$ has degree one and is adjacent only to $u_i$, so
\begin{equation}
 h_i^{(k)}
 =\frac{\vartheta}{\sqrt{d_{u_i}}}
   (M^{-1})_{u_i f_k}.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-frozen-response-entry}
\end{equation}
The frozen graph on $T$ is connected and $M$ is an irreducible SPD Stieltjes
matrix.  Its inverse is strictly positive, proving the first claim.

At $\vartheta=0$, $\alpha=1$ and
\begin{equation*}
 M(0)=\operatorname{diag}(I_A,(1+c_\eta)I_F).
\end{equation*}
The entries of $M^{-1}$ are analytic for all sufficiently small
$\vartheta$.  The only first-order anchor--petal entry in column $f_k$ is
\begin{equation*}
 (M^{-1})_{u_k f_k}
 =\frac{\vartheta}{\sqrt{d_{u_k}}(1+c_\eta)}
  +O_{n,\eta}(\vartheta^2),
\end{equation*}
whereas $(M^{-1})_{u_i f_k}=O_{n,\eta}(\vartheta^2)$ for $i\neq k$.
Substitution in \cref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-response-entry} proves
\cref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-matching-response,eq:notched-sun-frozen-off-response}.

The finite set of matching expansions implies, after shrinking
$\vartheta>0$ if needed, that every $h_k^{(k)}>\vartheta^{5/2}$.  The finite
set of off-matching expansions similarly gives
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{\ell\leq k}h_i^{(\ell)}
 \leq\frac12\vartheta^{5/2}
 \qquad(i>k).
\end{equation*}
All terms are positive.  Induction on $k$ now proves
\cref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-delta-trace}, including strictness at the gate
and separation from the transition band.

Under \textsc{EagerExactSlack}, every not-yet-emitted stored cell changes by
the strictly positive quantity $h_i^{(k)}$ (or its positive stream multiple).
An exact separately addressed cell cannot retain its old value, so the policy
performs at least one response-state read and one overwrite for every such
cell.  Summing the $n-k+1$ live cells proves
\cref{eq:notched-sun-eager-state-write-lower-bound}.  The fragment and output
counts follow directly from their declared interfaces.

For completeness, eliminating one degree-one $F$ row changes only its
incident anchor diagonal.  The remaining graph is the anchor cycle plus its
single chord.  Removing the wrap edge and chord leaves a tridiagonal path;
restoring them is a constant-rank symmetric update.  Exact path elimination,
a constant number of path solves, and a constant-size Schur solve therefore
construct the factor and apply it in $\Theta(n)$ exact cells.  Recovering the
leaves and contracting the $n$ exposed report incidences is also
$\Theta(n)$.  Counting these operations and the eager state invariant gives
\cref{eq:notched-sun-eager-eleven-vector}.  The final scope paragraph follows
because the lower bound uses the named invariant, not an information or
oracle restriction shared by the excluded alternatives.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A scale-certified delta reporter on the frozen notched sun]
\label{thm:notched-sun-scale-delta-reporter}
Use exactly the fixed face, exposed cut, complete shifted ladder, first-rung
matrix $M$, unit-fragment order, logical output columns, gate $g$, and band
$m=g/2$ of
\cref{prop:notched-sun-eager-slack-vector-obstruction}.  Thus $n\geq5$,
$\eta\in(0,1/8)$, $\vartheta=(1-\alpha)/2$, and
$c_\eta=(1+\eta)^2\eta^2$.  Impose the explicit near-one regime
\begin{equation}
 0<\vartheta\leq\frac1{1296n^2}.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-regime}
\end{equation}
Then, for every event $k$,
\begin{align}
 h_k^{(k)}
 &\geq\frac{\vartheta^2}{d_{u_k}(1+c_\eta)}
 >\vartheta^{5/2}=g,
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-matching}\\
 \sum_{\ell=1}^k h_i^{(\ell)}
 &\leq\frac{9k\vartheta^3}{1-3\vartheta}
 \leq\frac12\vartheta^{5/2}=g-m,
 \qquad i>k.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-future-tail}
\end{align}

Name the following promised-template partial reporter
\textsc{NotchedSunScaleDelta}.  Its preprocessing scans the frozen face,
queries the report degrees in a second adjacency round, verifies the complete
cycle--chord--petal--report template and label pairing, constructs the fixed
ladder, and checks \cref{eq:notched-sun-scale-regime}.  Its persistent state
after event $k$ is only
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf{State}_k
 =(\text{verified template and label map},\text{fixed ladder},k,
   \text{logical-column/sign metadata});
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-state}
\end{equation}
it contains no entry of $h^{(\ell)}$, no cumulative response $y^{[k]}$, and
no exterior slack cell.  At event $k+1$, it reads and verifies every supplied
first-rung fragment coordinate and its declared amplitude.  It then emits,
for each logical output column $q$, the delta record $(q,w_{k+1})$ and one
certificate asserting the universal statement
\begin{equation*}
 y_{q,i}^{[k+1]}\leq g-m
 \qquad\text{for every still-unreported }i>k+1.
\end{equation*}
The certificate is computationally available from the exact scalar right side
of \cref{eq:notched-sun-scale-future-tail} and the verified label order; it is
one record per logical column, not one scan or record per future label.

For $r$ nonnegative logical columns, event $k$ supplies $e_{f_k}$ in each of
the $p=r$ streams.  For a signed logical column, it supplies $2e_{f_k}$ in
its positive stream and $e_{f_k}$ in its negative stream, so $p=2r$ and the
exact logical response is $2y^{[k]}-y^{[k]}=y^{[k]}$.  The reporter checks
both signed-stream coordinates and amplitudes before combining their exact
template certificates; neither stream read is omitted.  Hence in the two
cases respectively
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}=pn=rn
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 \mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}=pn=2rn.
\end{equation*}

Generate and retain the complete $J$-rung ladder once, process the $p$ stream
records sequentially, and stream the $r$ logical replies.  In the shared
resource order, the complete epoch vector is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &(C_{\rm adj},R_{\rm adj},R_{\rm int},C_{\rm pre},C_{\rm ctl},C_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm resp},M_{\rm pers},M_{\rm tmp},C_{\rm mat},C_{\rm emit})\\
 =\bigl(&\Theta(n),2,n,\Theta(n+J+p),
 \Theta(\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}+rn+n),0,0,\\
 &\Theta(n+J+p),O(1),0,\Theta(rn)\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-delta-eleven-vector}
\end{equation}
Here preprocessing includes structural validation, label dictionaries,
ladder coefficients, gate/band generation, and stream-mode initialization.
Control includes every fragment read/check, event-order check, scalar tail
comparison, membership update, and construction of all delta and certificate
records.  There is no response application and no numerical response/slack
materialization.  Thus total additive exact-cell work is
$\Theta(n+J+p+\mathcal C_{\rm frag}^{\Sigma}+rn)$, which is output-sensitive
on this trace and strictly replaces the quadratic
\textsc{EagerExactSlack} state.

This GO is exact-real, fixed-face, fixed-cut, fixed-ladder, fixed-order, and
template-aware.  It is not an RPPR load or KKT chronology, a terminal
PPR/RPPR solver, a uniform fixed-$\alpha$ family as $n$ grows, a reporter for
arbitrary fragment amplitudes or orders, or an arbitrary high-cut-rank cyclic
partial flush.  It gives no coefficient-bit, word-RAM, rounding-stability, or
finite-precision guarantee.  In particular, it does not close the general
slack-normalized bank application problem: its future-safe certificate comes
from the proved template scale separation, not from a dynamic response query.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $N_T:=D_T^{-1/2}A_{TT}D_T^{-1/2}$ use the ambient graph degrees, and put
\begin{equation}
 B_0:=\operatorname{diag}(I_A,(1+c_\eta)I_F),
 \qquad
 R:=\operatorname{diag}(I_A,(1+2c_\eta)I_F)+N_T.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-blocks}
\end{equation}
The identities $\alpha=1-2\vartheta$ and
$Q_{TT}=(1-\vartheta)I_T-\vartheta N_T$ give the exact decomposition
\begin{equation}
 M=B_0-\vartheta R
 =B_0^{1/2}(I-\vartheta\mathsf K)B_0^{1/2},
 \qquad
 \mathsf K:=B_0^{-1/2}RB_0^{-1/2}.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-neumann-decomposition}
\end{equation}
Every entry of $\mathsf K$ is nonnegative; no positive-semidefinite order is
claimed.  The full normalized adjacency has operator norm at most one, hence
so does its principal block $N_T$.  Since $B_0^{-1/2}$ is a contraction and
$(1+2c_\eta)/(1+c_\eta)<2$,
\begin{equation*}
 \norm{\mathsf K}_2\leq3.
\end{equation*}
Therefore \cref{eq:notched-sun-scale-regime} permits the entrywise
nonnegative Neumann expansion
\begin{equation}
 M^{-1}
 =B_0^{-1/2}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}
   \vartheta^t\mathsf K^tB_0^{-1/2}.
 \label{eq:notched-sun-scale-neumann-series}
\end{equation}

The length-one term from $f_k$ to its unique anchor neighbor $u_k$ is
$\vartheta/[\sqrt{d_{u_k}}(1+c_\eta)]$.  All later terms are nonnegative, so
\cref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-response-entry} proves the first weak inequality
in \cref{eq:notched-sun-scale-matching}.  Moreover
$c_\eta<1/50$, $d_{u_k}\leq5$, and
$\sqrt\vartheta\leq1/(36n)\leq1/180<10/51$.  Hence
\begin{equation*}
 \frac1{d_{u_k}(1+c_\eta)}>\sqrt\vartheta,
\end{equation*}
which proves the strict gate inequality.

If $i\neq\ell$, the length-zero and length-one terms between $f_\ell$ and
$u_i$ vanish.  Using only the operator-norm bound on each remaining
entry---not a spectral sign claim---gives
\begin{equation*}
 (M^{-1})_{u_if_\ell}
 \leq\sum_{t=2}^{\infty}(3\vartheta)^t
 =\frac{9\vartheta^2}{1-3\vartheta}.
\end{equation*}
Equation~\eqref{eq:notched-sun-frozen-response-entry} and
$1/\sqrt{d_{u_i}}\leq1$ therefore give
$h_i^{(\ell)}\leq9\vartheta^3/(1-3\vartheta)$.  Sum over
$\ell\leq k$.  Because \cref{eq:notched-sun-scale-regime} implies
$1-3\vartheta\geq1/2$ and
$\sqrt\vartheta\leq1/(36n)$,
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{9k\vartheta^3}{1-3\vartheta}
 \leq18n\sqrt\vartheta\,\vartheta^{5/2}
 \leq\frac12\vartheta^{5/2},
